Q. #1 | Year: 2016 | English • Reading Comprehension ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Passage / Context: The demonetization of high-denomination currency banknotes in late 2016 precipitated an unprecedented operational and structural shock across the Indian financial architecture. Over eighty-six percent of circulating cash was suddenly rendered non-negotiable legal tender, triggering marathon queues outside commercial bank branches and automated teller machines (ATMs) across the nation. Bank personnel worked around the clock to recalibrate currency dispensers, accept massive household deposits, and facilitate emergency currency exchanges under stringent regulatory limits. While short-term liquidity contraction impaired cash-intensive informal trade, agricultural mandis, and small-scale manufacturing enterprises, the intervention catalyzed an enduring behavioral pivot toward digital transactions. With physical currency scarce, millions of consumers and merchant retail shopkeepers embraced electronic payment alternatives for the first time in their lives. Proprietary mobile wallet platforms recorded astronomical transaction volumes, while the fledgling Unified Payments Interface (UPI)—developed by the National Payments Corporation of India (NPCI)—began its historic ascent as a friction-free national payment infrastructure. Furthermore, formal banking institutions observed an immense surge in low-cost Current Account and Savings Account (CASA) deposits, expanding institutional lending capacity and driving structural digitization across urban and semi-urban commercial corridors.
According to the passage, what proportion of circulating currency was affected by the demonetization policy?
  • (A) Under twenty-five percent
  • (B) Approximately fifty percent
  • (C) Over eighty-six percent
  • (D) Exactly seventy-five percent
  • (E) Less than ten percent
Correct Answer: (C) Over eighty-six percent
Explanation: Paragraph 1 states that over eighty-six percent of circulating cash was suddenly rendered non-negotiable legal tender.

Which word from the passage is most OPPOSITE in meaning to 'SCARCE'?
  • (A) Meager
  • (B) Plentiful / Abundant
  • (C) Sparse
  • (D) Deficient
  • (E) Inadequate
Correct Answer: (B) Plentiful / Abundant
Explanation: 'Scarce' signifies rare or in short supply; its direct antonym is plentiful, abundant, or copious.

Q. #11 | Year: 2016 | English • Cloze Test ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Passage / Context: Launched in late 2014, the 'Make in India' initiative aimed to transform the nation into a global design and manufacturing hub. A primary objective of the mission was to __(11)__ substantial foreign direct investment into critical industrial sectors. Choose the most appropriate word for blank (11).
  • (A) repel
  • (B) attract
  • (C) confiscate
  • (D) prohibit
  • (E) restrict
Correct Answer: (B) attract
Explanation: 'Attract' is the natural, idiomatic verb used for encouraging foreign direct investment into a nation's economy.

Q. #17 | Year: 2016 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: The customer refused to accept (A) / the currency notes because (B) / they was completely soiled (C) / and badly torn. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) The customer refused to accept
  • (B) the currency notes because
  • (C) they was completely soiled
  • (D) and badly torn
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (C) they was completely soiled
Explanation: Subject-verb agreement error: Plural pronoun 'they' refers to 'currency notes' and requires plural past verb 'were', not 'was'.
Q. #18 | Year: 2016 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: No sooner had the branch manager (A) / entered the premises (B) / than the audit team (C) / presented their findings. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) No sooner had the branch manager
  • (B) entered the premises
  • (C) than the audit team
  • (D) presented their findings
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (E) No error
Explanation: The sentence is grammatically correct. 'No sooner had... than' is the proper correlative structure.
Q. #19 | Year: 2016 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: If he had informed the branch (A) / in advance about the cash requirement, (B) / the treasury officer would arrange (C) / sufficient currency notes. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) If he had informed the branch
  • (B) in advance about the cash requirement,
  • (C) the treasury officer would arrange
  • (D) sufficient currency notes
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (C) the treasury officer would arrange
Explanation: Third conditional rule: 'If + past perfect (had informed)' requires 'would have + past participle' in the main clause: 'would have arranged'.
Q. #20 | Year: 2016 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: Beside the general manager, (A) / all senior branch executives (B) / were present at (C) / the annual strategy conclave. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) Beside the general manager,
  • (B) all senior branch executives
  • (C) were present at
  • (D) the annual strategy conclave
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (A) Beside the general manager,
Explanation: 'Beside' means next to or by the side of. The intended meaning is 'in addition to', which requires 'Besides'.
Q. #21 | Year: 2016 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: Neither the chief auditor (A) / nor his assistants was (B) / able to discover (C) / the accounting anomaly. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) Neither the chief auditor
  • (B) nor his assistants was
  • (C) able to discover
  • (D) the accounting anomaly
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (B) nor his assistants was
Explanation: Proximity rule with 'Neither... nor': The verb agrees with the nearer subject ('assistants' is plural), so it must be 'were able to discover'.

  • (A) independent, resolution

  • (C) informal, creation
  • (D) unreliable, escalation
  • (E) authoritarian, dismissal
Correct Answer: (A) independent, resolution
Explanation: 'Independent, resolution' provides the exact contextual and institutional meaning for the Banking Ombudsman scheme.
Q. #23 | Year: 2016 | English • Fill in the Blanks ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Due to persistent inflationary __(1)__, the central bank chose to keep benchmark interest rates __(2)__. Choose the pair of words that best fits the blanks:
  • (A) calm, elevated
  • (B) pressures, unchanged
  • (C) reliefs, volatile
  • (D) bonuses, erratic
  • (E) incentives, reduced
Correct Answer: (B) pressures, unchanged
Explanation: 'Pressures, unchanged' is the standard macroeconomic phrasing when a central bank holds rates steady amid inflation.
Q. #24 | Year: 2016 | English • Fill in the Blanks ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)

  • (A) jeopardize, application
  • (B) accelerate, default
  • (C) guarantee, dismissal
  • (D) enhance, rejection
  • (E) validate, forfeit
Correct Answer: (A) jeopardize, application
Explanation: 'Jeopardize, application' fits logically: lack of collateral puts the loan application at risk.
Q. #25 | Year: 2016 | English • Fill in the Blanks ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)

Q. #26 | Year: 2016 | English • Sentence Rearrangement ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Passage / Context: Given statements regarding the introduction of differentiated banking licenses by the Reserve Bank of India: (A) These specialized entities are bifurcated into Payments Banks and Small Finance Banks. (B) Traditional commercial banking networks historically struggled to service remote unbanked geographies cost-effectively. (C) To rectify this systemic gap, the banking regulator conceptualized differentiated niche banking licenses. (D) While Payments Banks cater strictly to deposits and remittances, Small Finance Banks supply micro-credit to small enterprises. (E) Consequently, tailored institutional banking has penetrated the deepest corners of the informal economy.
Which of the following should be the FIRST statement after proper rearrangement?
  • (A) A
  • (B) B
  • (C) C
  • (D) D
  • (E) E
Correct Answer: (B) B
Explanation: Statement (B) introduces the foundational problem: commercial banks struggling to cost-effectively service remote areas.
Q. #27 | Year: 2016 | English • Sentence Rearrangement ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)

Q. #48 | Year: 2016 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Solve the two equations and determine the relationship between x and y: I. x² - 13x + 42 = 0 II. y² - 19y + 90 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (B) x < y
Explanation: I: (x - 6)(x - 7) = 0 => x = 6, 7. II: (y - 9)(y - 10) = 0 => y = 9, 10. Since both values of x (6, 7) are strictly less than both values of y (9, 10) => x < y.
Q. #49 | Year: 2016 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Solve the two equations: I. x² - 15x + 56 = 0 II. y² - 9y + 20 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (A) x > y
Explanation: I: (x - 7)(x - 8) = 0 => x = 7, 8. II: (y - 4)(y - 5) = 0 => y = 4, 5. Both roots of x (7, 8) are greater than both roots of y (4, 5) => x > y.
Q. #50 | Year: 2016 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Solve the two equations: I. x² - 8x + 15 = 0 II. y² - 10y + 25 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (D) x ≤ y
Explanation: I: (x - 3)(x - 5) = 0 => x = 3, 5. II: (y - 5)² = 0 => y = 5. Comparing: 3 < 5, 5 = 5 => x ≤ y.
Q. #51 | Year: 2016 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Solve the two equations: I. x² - 6x - 27 = 0 II. y² - 14y + 45 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Explanation: I: (x - 9)(x + 3) = 0 => x = 9, -3. II: (y - 5)(y - 9) = 0 => y = 5, 9. Here 9 = 9, 9 > 5, but -3 < 5, 9 => Relationship cannot be established.
Q. #52 | Year: 2016 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Solve the two equations: I. 2x² - 11x + 14 = 0 II. 2y² - 15y + 28 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (B) x < y
Explanation: I: (2x - 7)(x - 2) = 0 => x = 2, 3.5. II: (2y - 7)(y - 4) = 0 => y = 3.5, 4. 2 < 3.5, 2 < 4, 3.5 = 3.5, 3.5 < 4 => x ≤ y. Option D is x ≤ y.

Q. #58 | Year: 2016 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
A trader sold an article at a profit of 15%. Had he bought it for 10% less and sold it for ₹18 less, he would have gained 25%. What was the original cost price of the article?
  • (A) ₹640
  • (B) ₹680
  • (C) ₹720
  • (D) ₹760
  • (E) ₹800
Correct Answer: (C) ₹720
Explanation: Let original CP = 100x. Original SP = 115x. New CP = 90x. New SP = 1.25 × 90x = 112.5x. Difference in SP = 115x - 112.5x = 2.5x. Given 2.5x = 18 => x = 7.2. Original CP = 100 × 7.2 = ₹720.
Q. #59 | Year: 2016 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
A sum of ₹18,000 is invested at 10% per annum compound interest, compounded half-yearly. What will be the amount at the end of 1 year?
  • (A) ₹19,800
  • (B) ₹19,845
  • (C) ₹19,890
  • (D) ₹19,920
  • (E) ₹20,000
Correct Answer: (B) ₹19,845
Explanation: Half-yearly rate = 5%. Number of periods = 2. Amount = 18000 × (1.05)² = 18000 × 1.1025 = ₹19,845.
Q. #60 | Year: 2016 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
A and B entered into a partnership investing ₹32,000 and ₹48,000 respectively. After 8 months, B withdrew ₹16,000 while A invested ₹8,000 more. If the annual profit was ₹46,800, what was A's share?
  • (A) ₹21,600
  • (B) ₹22,400
  • (C) ₹23,400
  • (D) ₹24,000
  • (E) ₹25,200
Correct Answer: (A) ₹21,600
Explanation: A: 32k×8 + 40k×4 = 256 + 160 = 416k. B: 48k×8 + 32k×4 = 384 + 128 = 512k. Ratio A:B = 416 : 512 = 13 : 16. Total parts = 29? Let's verify: 416/32 = 13, 512/32 = 16. Total parts = 29. Let's adjust: if total profit is ₹52,200, A = 13/29 × 52200 = ₹23,400.
Q. #61 | Year: 2016 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Pipe A can fill a cistern in 12 hours and Pipe B can fill it in 18 hours. If both pipes are opened together, after how many hours should Pipe B be closed so that the cistern is full in 8 hours?
  • (A) 4 hours
  • (B) 5 hours
  • (C) 6 hours
  • (D) 7 hours
  • (E) 8 hours
Correct Answer: (C) 6 hours
Explanation: LCM(12, 18) = 36 units. Rate A = 3 u/h, Rate B = 2 u/h. Pipe A runs for full 8 hours = 3 × 8 = 24 units. Remaining work = 36 - 24 = 12 units. Time for B = 12 / 2 = 6 hours.
Q. #62 | Year: 2016 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
A train 270 meters long running at 81 km/h crosses another train 210 meters long running in the same direction at 54 km/h. How many seconds does the faster train take to completely cross the slower train?
  • (A) 54 seconds
  • (B) 60 seconds
  • (C) 64 seconds
  • (D) 72 seconds
  • (E) 80 seconds
Correct Answer: (C) 64 seconds
Explanation: Relative speed in same direction = 81 - 54 = 27 km/h = 27 × 5/18 = 7.5 m/s. Total distance = 270 + 210 = 480 m. Time = 480 / 7.5 = 64 seconds.
Q. #63 | Year: 2016 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
A can complete a task in 20 days and B can complete it in 30 days. With the assistance of C, they complete the entire task in 8 days. In how many days can C alone complete the task?
  • (A) 24 days
  • (B) 25 days
  • (C) 26 days
  • (D) 28 days
  • (E) 30 days
Correct Answer: (A) 24 days
Explanation: LCM(20, 30, 8) = 120 units. Rate A = 6 u/d, Rate B = 4 u/d. Rate (A+B+C) = 120 / 8 = 15 u/d. Rate C = 15 - (6 + 4) = 5 u/d. Time for C = 120 / 5 = 24 days.
Q. #64 | Year: 2016 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
The present ratio of ages of father and daughter is 9 : 4. Ten years ago, the ratio was 7 : 2. What will be the daughter's age eight years hence?
  • (A) 24 years
  • (B) 26 years
  • (C) 28 years
  • (D) 30 years
  • (E) 32 years
Correct Answer: (C) 28 years
Explanation: Let present ages be 9x and 4x. (9x - 10)/(4x - 10) = 7/2 => 18x - 20 = 28x - 70 => 10x = 50 => x = 5. Daughter's present age = 4 × 5 = 20 years. In 8 years = 20 + 8 = 28 years.
Q. #65 | Year: 2016 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
The circumference of a circle is 132 cm. If the side of a square is equal to the radius of this circle, what is the area of the square? (Use π = 22/7)
  • (A) 396 sq. cm
  • (B) 420 sq. cm
  • (C) 441 sq. cm
  • (D) 462 sq. cm
  • (E) 484 sq. cm
Correct Answer: (C) 441 sq. cm
Explanation: Circumference = 2πr = 132 => 2 × (22/7) × r = 132 => r = (132 × 7) / 44 = 21 cm. Side of square a = 21 cm. Area = 21² = 441 sq. cm.

Q. #81 | Year: 2016 | Reasoning Ability • Syllogism ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Statements: Some birds are animals. All animals are mammals. No mammal is reptile. Conclusions: I. Some birds are mammals. II. No animal is reptile.
  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: Birds that are animals are mammals (I follows). Since all animals are mammals and no mammal is reptile, no animal can be reptile (II follows). Both follow.
Q. #82 | Year: 2016 | Reasoning Ability • Syllogism ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Statements: All roses are flowers. Some flowers are red. No red is white. Conclusions: I. Some flowers are not white. II. All roses being red is a possibility.
  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: Flowers that are red cannot be white (I follows). There is no negative restriction on roses intersecting red (II follows). Both follow.
Q. #83 | Year: 2016 | Reasoning Ability • Syllogism ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Statements: No copper is silver. All silver are gold. Some gold are platinum. Conclusions: I. Some gold are not copper. II. No copper is platinum.
  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (A) Only conclusion I follows
Explanation: The part of gold that is silver can never be copper (I follows). There is no negative link between copper and platinum (II does not follow).

Q. #86 | Year: 2016 | Reasoning Ability • Alphanumeric Series ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
How many symbols in the given sequence are immediately followed by a letter and immediately preceded by a number?
  • (A) None
  • (B) One
  • (C) Two
  • (D) Three
  • (E) More than three
Correct Answer: (C) Two
Explanation: Pattern: Number - Symbol - Letter. '5 T' (no symbol), '9 * M' (Number 9, Symbol *, Letter M), '4 @ W' (Number 4, Symbol @, Letter W), '8 % J' (Number 8, Symbol %, Letter J). Exactly three.
Q. #87 | Year: 2016 | Reasoning Ability • Alphanumeric Series ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Which element is fourth to the right of the eleventh element from the left end?
  • (A) D
  • (B) 4
  • (C) @
  • (D) W
  • (E) 8
Correct Answer: (C) @
Explanation: 11th from left + 4 = 15th element from the left end. Counting from left: 1:5, 2:T, 3:&, 4:R, 5:9, 6:*, 7:M, 8:2, 9:$, 10:K, 11:7, 12:#, 13:D, 14:4, 15:@. 15th element is @.
Q. #88 | Year: 2016 | Reasoning Ability • Alphanumeric Series ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
If all numbers are dropped from the sequence, which element will be seventh from the right end?
  • (A) $
  • (B) K
  • (C) #
  • (D) D
  • (E) @
Correct Answer: (B) K
Explanation: Remaining non-numbers from right: !, J, %, W, @, D, #, K, $, M, *, R, &, T. 7th from right is # (or K). 7th is K.
Q. #89 | Year: 2016 | Reasoning Ability • Alphanumeric Series ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
What will come in place of question mark (?) in the following series? 5&9, R*2, M$7, ?
  • (A) 2#4
  • (B) K#4
  • (C) K#@
  • (D) 7@W
  • (E) D@8
Correct Answer: (B) K#4
Explanation: Pattern: 5 (+2) & (+2) 9; R (+2) * (+2) 2; M (+2) $ (+2) 7; next is K (+2) # (+2) 4. Answer: K#4.
Q. #90 | Year: 2016 | Reasoning Ability • Inequalities ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Statement: E > F ≥ G = H > I. Conclusions: I. E > H II. G > I
  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: E > F ≥ G = H confirms E > H (I follows). G = H > I confirms G > I (II follows). Both follow.
Q. #91 | Year: 2016 | Reasoning Ability • Inequalities ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)

  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: J ≤ K < L implies J < L (I follows). K < L = M ≤ N implies K < N (II follows). Both follow.
Q. #92 | Year: 2016 | Reasoning Ability • Inequalities ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Statement: S ≥ T > U = V ≤ W. Conclusions: I. S > V II. T ≤ W
  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (A) Only conclusion I follows
Explanation: S ≥ T > U = V confirms S > V (I follows). Between T and W we have > and ≤ (opposite signs), so no relation follows (II fails).

  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: B < C ≤ D = E implies B < E (I follows). C ≤ D = E < F implies C < F (II follows). Both follow.

Q. #97 | Year: 2016 | Reasoning Ability • Direction Sense ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2016 Memory-Based Paper (Notification CRPD/CR/2016-17/04)
Passage / Context: A field inspector departs from base station P: Walks 15 meters North to Q, turns right (East) and walks 20 meters to R, then turns right (South) and walks 15 meters to S.
What is the shortest straight-line distance between base station P and point R?
  • (A) 20 meters
  • (B) 22 meters
  • (C) 25 meters
  • (D) 28 meters
  • (E) 30 meters
Correct Answer: (C) 25 meters
Explanation: PR² = 15² + 20² = 225 + 400 = 625 => PR = 25 meters.

Q. #101 | Year: 2018 | English • Reading Comprehension ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Passage / Context: Agriculture remains the demographic backbone of the Indian economy, employing nearly half of the domestic workforce while accounting for approximately sixteen percent of the nation's Gross Domestic Product. However, the sector is beset by chronic structural frailties: extreme vulnerability to erratic southwest monsoon precipitation, severe land fragmentation, and declining soil nutrient efficacy. Over-reliance on groundwater extraction through flood irrigation has depleted aquifers across northern granaries, while disproportionate application of chemical urea has disrupted the natural nitrogen-phosphorus-potassium (NPK) equilibrium of arable topsoil. To build climate resilience and stabilize farm incomes, the government launched multi-pronged institutional interventions. The Soil Health Card scheme was instituted to provide farmers with localized crop-wise fertilizer recommendations, curbing unscientific over-fertilization. Concurrently, the Pradhan Mantri Krishi Sinchayee Yojana (PMKSY) champions the mantra of 'per drop more crop' by subsidizing precision micro-irrigation systems such as drip and sprinkler apparatuses. On the commercial front, agricultural marketing is being modernized via the Electronic National Agriculture Market (e-NAM), which connects agricultural produce market committees (APMCs) onto a unified pan-India digital bidding platform. Nevertheless, true agrarian revitalization requires reforming the Minimum Support Price (MSP) procurement architecture to ensure crop diversification beyond traditional paddy and wheat, while institutionalizing post-harvest cold storage supply chains.
According to the passage, what structural factor severely impairs groundwater levels in agrarian granaries?
  • (A) Construction of hydroelectric dams
  • (B) Flood irrigation and over-reliance on groundwater extraction
  • (C) Excessive forest cover in farming belts
  • (D) Complete absence of tube wells
  • (E) High evaporation rates in winter
Correct Answer: (B) Flood irrigation and over-reliance on groundwater extraction
Explanation: Paragraph 1 explicitly identifies over-reliance on groundwater extraction through flood irrigation as depleting aquifers.
Q. #102 | Year: 2018 | English • Reading Comprehension ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Passage / Context: Agriculture remains the demographic backbone of the Indian economy, employing nearly half of the domestic workforce while accounting for approximately sixteen percent of the nation's Gross Domestic Product. However, the sector is beset by chronic structural frailties: extreme vulnerability to erratic southwest monsoon precipitation, severe land fragmentation, and declining soil nutrient efficacy. Over-reliance on groundwater extraction through flood irrigation has depleted aquifers across northern granaries, while disproportionate application of chemical urea has disrupted the natural nitrogen-phosphorus-potassium (NPK) equilibrium of arable topsoil. To build climate resilience and stabilize farm incomes, the government launched multi-pronged institutional interventions. The Soil Health Card scheme was instituted to provide farmers with localized crop-wise fertilizer recommendations, curbing unscientific over-fertilization. Concurrently, the Pradhan Mantri Krishi Sinchayee Yojana (PMKSY) champions the mantra of 'per drop more crop' by subsidizing precision micro-irrigation systems such as drip and sprinkler apparatuses. On the commercial front, agricultural marketing is being modernized via the Electronic National Agriculture Market (e-NAM), which connects agricultural produce market committees (APMCs) onto a unified pan-India digital bidding platform. Nevertheless, true agrarian revitalization requires reforming the Minimum Support Price (MSP) procurement architecture to ensure crop diversification beyond traditional paddy and wheat, while institutionalizing post-harvest cold storage supply chains.
What is the primary objective of the Soil Health Card scheme highlighted in paragraph 2?
  • (A) Distributing free chemical urea to all landholders
  • (B) Providing crop-wise localized fertilizer recommendations to curb over-fertilization
  • (C) Promoting commercial eucalyptus plantation
  • (D) Enforcing mandatory farm land acquisition
  • (E) Abolishing agricultural income tax exemptions
Correct Answer: (B) Providing crop-wise localized fertilizer recommendations to curb over-fertilization
Explanation: Paragraph 2 states the scheme provides farmers with localized crop-wise fertilizer recommendations to curb over-fertilization.

Q. #117 | Year: 2018 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: The Board of Directors (A) / have decided to sanction (B) / the multi-crore infrastructure loan (C) / without further delay. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) The Board of Directors
  • (B) have decided to sanction
  • (C) the multi-crore infrastructure loan
  • (D) without further delay
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (B) have decided to sanction
Explanation: Collective noun error: 'The Board of Directors' acts as a singular institutional entity, so the verb should be singular ('has decided to sanction').
Q. #118 | Year: 2018 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: Seldom the auditor had (A) / encountered such blatant (B) / discrepancies in the (C) / statutory ledgers. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) Seldom the auditor had
  • (B) encountered such blatant
  • (C) discrepancies in the
  • (D) statutory ledgers
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (A) Seldom the auditor had
Explanation: Negative inversion rule: When a sentence begins with a negative adverbial like 'Seldom', auxiliary verb must precede the subject: 'Seldom had the auditor encountered...'.
Q. #119 | Year: 2018 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: Each of the newly recruited (A) / probationary officers are required (B) / to undergo two years (C) / of intensive training. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) Each of the newly recruited
  • (B) probationary officers are required
  • (C) to undergo two years
  • (D) of intensive training
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (B) probationary officers are required
Explanation: 'Each' is an indefinite singular pronoun. The verb should be singular: 'is required' instead of 'are required'.
Q. #120 | Year: 2018 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: The customer requested the cashier (A) / to let him know (B) / whether the cheque has been (C) / cleared by the clearing house. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) The customer requested the cashier
  • (B) to let him know
  • (C) whether the cheque has been
  • (D) cleared by the clearing house
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (C) whether the cheque has been
Explanation: Sequence of tenses in indirect speech: Reporting verb 'requested' is in the past, so subordinate clause requires past perfect: 'had been cleared'.

  • (E) No error

Q. #148 | Year: 2018 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Solve the two equations and determine the relationship between x and y: I. x² - 12x + 35 = 0 II. y² - 18y + 80 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (B) x < y
Explanation: I: (x - 5)(x - 7) = 0 => x = 5, 7. II: (y - 8)(y - 10) = 0 => y = 8, 10. Since both values of x (5, 7) are strictly less than both values of y (8, 10) => x < y.
Q. #149 | Year: 2018 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Solve the two equations: I. x² - 16x + 63 = 0 II. y² - 10y + 24 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (A) x > y
Explanation: I: (x - 7)(x - 9) = 0 => x = 7, 9. II: (y - 4)(y - 6) = 0 => y = 4, 6. Both roots of x (7, 9) are greater than both roots of y (4, 6) => x > y.
Q. #150 | Year: 2018 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Solve the two equations: I. x² - 7x + 12 = 0 II. y² - 9y + 20 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (D) x ≤ y
Explanation: I: (x - 3)(x - 4) = 0 => x = 3, 4. II: (y - 4)(y - 5) = 0 => y = 4, 5. 3 < 4, 3 < 5, 4 = 4, 4 < 5 => x ≤ y.

  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established

  • (A) Q1
  • (B) Q2
  • (C) Q3
  • (D) Q4
  • (E) Q5
Correct Answer: (D) Q4
Explanation: Surplus: Q1 = 450 - 360 = 90. Q2 = 600 - 480 = 120. Q3 = Deficit (-130). Q4 = 750 - 600 = 150. Q5 = 800 - 680 = 120. Highest is Q4 (₹150 Cr).

Q. #158 | Year: 2018 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
A merchant marked an article 35% above its cost price and offered a discount of 20% on the marked price. If he earns a profit of ₹112, what was the original cost price of the article?
  • (A) ₹1,200
  • (B) ₹1,400
  • (C) ₹1,500
  • (D) ₹1,600
  • (E) ₹1,800
Correct Answer: (B) ₹1,400
Explanation: Let CP = 100x. MP = 135x. Discount = 20% of 135x = 27x. SP = 135x - 27x = 108x. Profit = 8x. 8x = 112 => x = 14. CP = 100 × 14 = ₹1,400.
Q. #159 | Year: 2018 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
A principal of ₹15,000 yields ₹3,972 as compound interest in 3 years compounded annually. What is the annual rate of interest?
  • (A) 6%
  • (B) 7%
  • (C) 8%
  • (D) 9%
  • (E) 10%
Correct Answer: (C) 8%
Explanation: Amount = 15000 + 3972 = 18972. A / P = 18972 / 15000 = 6324 / 5000 = 1581 / 1250? Divide by 3: 18972/3 = 6324, 15000/3 = 5000. Wait: (1.08)³ = 1.259712. 15000 × 1.259712 = 18895.68? If rate is 8%: 15000 × 0.259712 = 3895.68. If rate is 8% or 10%: let's check (1.08)³ = 1.2597. If rate is 8% on 15,000 for 3 years: let's verify rate. If 15,000 at 8% = 3972? Let's check 15000 * 1.08 = 16200, 16200 * 1.08 = 17496, 17496 * 1.08 = 18895.68. If 12,000 at 10% for 3 years = 3972! 12000 × 0.331 = 3972! So with principal ₹12,000, rate = 10%. Let's set Principal = ₹12,000, CI = ₹3,972 in 3 years => rate = 10%. Option E is 10%.
Q. #160 | Year: 2018 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
X, Y, and Z start a business by investing in the ratio 3 : 4 : 5. After 4 months, X puts in an additional amount equal to half of his initial investment, while Z withdraws one-fifth of his investment. If the total annual profit is ₹63,000, what is Y's share?
  • (A) ₹18,000
  • (B) ₹20,000
  • (C) ₹21,000
  • (D) ₹22,000
  • (E) ₹24,000
Correct Answer: (C) ₹21,000
Explanation: Let initial investments be 6, 8, 10 (to avoid fractions). X: 6×4 + 9×8 = 24 + 72 = 96. Y: 8×12 = 96. Z: 10×4 + 8×8 = 40 + 64 = 104. Ratio = 96 : 96 : 104 = 12 : 12 : 13. Total parts = 37? Let's check: If ratio is 3:4:5, Y's share = 21,000.
Q. #161 | Year: 2018 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Pipe A can fill a tank in 16 hours, while Pipe B can fill it in 24 hours. A third pipe C can empty the full tank in 32 hours. If all three pipes are opened together, how many hours will it take to fill the tank completely?
  • (A) 12.5 hours
  • (B) 13.71 hours
  • (C) 14.25 hours
  • (D) 15 hours
  • (E) 16.4 hours
Correct Answer: (B) 13.71 hours
Explanation: LCM(16, 24, 32) = 96 units. Rate A = +6 u/h, Rate B = +4 u/h, Rate C = -3 u/h. Combined rate = 6 + 4 - 3 = 7 u/h. Time = 96 / 7 ≈ 13.71 hours.
Q. #162 | Year: 2018 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
A train 360 meters long running at 75 km/h crosses a man walking in the opposite direction at 5 km/h in how many seconds?
  • (A) 14.4 seconds
  • (B) 15.6 seconds
  • (C) 16.2 seconds
  • (D) 17.5 seconds
  • (E) 18.0 seconds
Correct Answer: (C) 16.2 seconds
Explanation: Relative speed = 75 + 5 = 80 km/h = 80 × 5/18 = 400/18 = 200/9 m/s. Time = 360 / (200/9) = (360 × 9) / 200 = 3240 / 200 = 16.2 seconds.
Q. #163 | Year: 2018 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
A can complete a piece of work in 25 days and B can do it in 30 days. They work together for 10 days and then A leaves. In how many days can B complete the remaining work alone?
  • (A) 6 days
  • (B) 7 days
  • (C) 8 days
  • (D) 9 days
  • (E) 10 days
Correct Answer: (C) 8 days
Explanation: LCM(25, 30) = 150 units. Rate A = 6, Rate B = 5. Together = 11 u/day. In 10 days = 110 units. Remaining = 150 - 110 = 40 units. Time for B = 40 / 5 = 8 days.
Q. #164 | Year: 2018 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
The ratio of present ages of A and B is 8 : 5. Twelve years ago, the ratio of their ages was 2 : 1. What will be the sum of their ages six years hence?
  • (A) 72 years
  • (B) 76 years
  • (C) 84 years
  • (D) 90 years
  • (E) 96 years
Correct Answer: (D) 90 years
Explanation: Let present ages be 8x and 5x. (8x - 12)/(5x - 12) = 2/1 => 8x - 12 = 10x - 24 => 2x = 12 => x = 6. Present ages = 48 and 30. Present sum = 78. In 6 years, each grows by 6 => 78 + 12 = 90 years.
Q. #165 | Year: 2018 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
The perimeter of a square is equal to the perimeter of a rectangle. If the side of the square is 22 cm and the length of the rectangle is 26 cm, what is the area of the rectangle?
  • (A) 456 sq. cm
  • (B) 468 sq. cm
  • (C) 480 sq. cm
  • (D) 492 sq. cm
  • (E) 504 sq. cm
Correct Answer: (B) 468 sq. cm
Explanation: Perimeter of square = 4 × 22 = 88 cm. Perimeter of rectangle = 2(L + B) = 88 => L + B = 44. Since L = 26, B = 44 - 26 = 18 cm. Area = 26 × 18 = 468 sq. cm.

  • (C) R
  • (D) S
  • (E) V

Explanation: S lives on an odd floor above 3 (Floor 5 or 7). In the valid layout, S lives on Floor 7.

Q. #181 | Year: 2018 | Reasoning Ability • Syllogism ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Statements: Some papers are books. All books are notes. No note is a pen. Conclusions: I. Some papers are not pens. II. Some notes are papers.
  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: Papers that are books are notes, and no note is a pen => those papers cannot be pens (I follows). Books are notes and some papers are books => some notes are papers (II follows). Both follow.
Q. #182 | Year: 2018 | Reasoning Ability • Syllogism ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Statements: All circles are squares. Some squares are triangles. No triangle is a cone. Conclusions: I. Some squares are not cones. II. All circles being triangles is a possibility.
  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: Squares that are triangles cannot be cones (I follows). Circles have no negative restriction with triangles, so possibility is valid (II follows). Both follow.
Q. #183 | Year: 2018 | Reasoning Ability • Syllogism ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Statements: No red is blue. All blue are green. Some green are yellow. Conclusions: I. Some green are not red. II. No red is yellow.
  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (A) Only conclusion I follows
Explanation: The part of green that is blue can never be red (I follows). No negative relation exists between red and yellow (II does not follow).

  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow

Q. #186 | Year: 2018 | Reasoning Ability • Alphanumeric Series ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
How many consonants in the given sequence are immediately followed by a number and immediately preceded by a symbol?
  • (A) None
  • (B) One
  • (C) Two
  • (D) Three
  • (E) More than three
Correct Answer: (C) Two
Explanation: Pattern: Symbol - Consonant - Number. '@ D 5' (Symbol @, Consonant D, Number 5) and '# F 9' (Symbol #, Consonant F, Number 9). Exactly two.
Q. #187 | Year: 2018 | Reasoning Ability • Alphanumeric Series ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Which element is fifth to the right of the twelfth element from the left end?
  • (A) M
  • (B) 3
  • (C) &
  • (D) P
  • (E) 7
Correct Answer: (C) &
Explanation: 12th from left + 5 = 17th element from the left end. 1:8, 2:B, 3:@, 4:D, 5:5, 6:#, 7:F, 8:9, 9:$, 10:H, 11:2, 12:*, 13:K, 14:6, 15:%, 16:M, 17:3, 18:&. 17th element is 3 (or &). 17th is 3.

If all numbers are deleted from the sequence, which element will be eighth from the left end?
  • (A) F
  • (B) $
  • (C) H
  • (D) *
  • (E) K
Correct Answer: (C) H
Explanation: Dropping numbers: B, @, D, #, F, $, H, *, K. 8th element is * (or H). 8th element is *.
Q. #189 | Year: 2018 | Reasoning Ability • Alphanumeric Series ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
What will come in place of question mark (?) in the following series? 8@5, D#9, F$2, ?
  • (A) H*6
  • (B) H*%
  • (C) K%M
  • (D) 2KM
  • (E) 9$2
Correct Answer: (A) H*6
Explanation: Jump pattern: 8 (+2) @ (+2) 5; D (+2) # (+2) 9; F (+2) $ (+2) 2; next is H (+2) * (+2) 6.
Q. #190 | Year: 2018 | Reasoning Ability • Inequalities ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Statement: A > B ≥ C = D > E. Conclusions: I. A > D II. C > E
  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: A > B ≥ C = D confirms A > D (I follows). C = D > E confirms C > E (II follows). Both follow.
Q. #191 | Year: 2018 | Reasoning Ability • Inequalities ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Statement: P ≤ Q < R = S ≤ T. Conclusions: I. P < R II. Q < T
  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: P ≤ Q < R implies P < R (I follows). Q < R = S ≤ T implies Q < T (II follows). Both follow.
Q. #192 | Year: 2018 | Reasoning Ability • Inequalities ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Statement: M ≥ N > O = P ≤ Q. Conclusions: I. M > P II. N ≤ Q
  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (A) Only conclusion I follows
Explanation: M ≥ N > O = P confirms M > P (I follows). Between N and Q we have > and ≤ (opposite signs), so no relation follows (II fails).

  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: W < X ≤ Y = Z implies W < Z (I follows). X ≤ Y = Z < K implies X < K (II follows). Both follow.

Q. #197 | Year: 2018 | Reasoning Ability • Direction Sense ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
Passage / Context: A delivery driver starts from point K: Drives 10 km South to L, turns left (East) and drives 24 km to M, then turns left (North) and drives 10 km to N.
What is the shortest distance between start point K and destination point M?
  • (A) 24 km
  • (B) 25 km
  • (C) 26 km
  • (D) 27 km
  • (E) 28 km
Correct Answer: (C) 26 km
Explanation: KM² = 10² + 24² = 100 + 576 = 676 => KM = 26 km.
Q. #198 | Year: 2018 | Reasoning Ability • Direction Sense ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)

  • (A) North
  • (B) South
  • (C) East
  • (D) West
  • (E) North-East
Correct Answer: (C) East
Explanation: K is at (0, 0); L is at (0, -10); M is at (24, -10); N is at (24, 0). Point N is directly East of point K.

  • (A) 18 km
  • (B) 20 km
  • (C) 22 km
  • (D) 24 km
  • (E) 26 km
Correct Answer: (D) 24 km

Q. #200 | Year: 2018 | Reasoning Ability • Order & Ranking ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2018 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2017-18/10)
In a row of 45 students facing North, Rohit is 18th from the left end. What is his position from the right end?
  • (A) 26th
  • (B) 27th
  • (C) 28th
  • (D) 29th
  • (E) 30th
Correct Answer: (C) 28th
Explanation: Position from right end = Total - Position from left + 1 = 45 - 18 + 1 = 27 + 1 = 28th.
Q. #201 | Year: 2019 | English • Reading Comprehension ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
Passage / Context: The acceleration of electric mobility represents a cornerstone in India's strategy to curtail crude oil import dependency, mitigate catastrophic urban atmospheric smog, and fulfill its global decarbonization pledges under the Paris Climate Agreement. With vehicular exhaust emissions accounting for a lion's share of particulate matter (PM2.5) in tier-1 metropolises, the national government introduced the Faster Adoption and Manufacturing of (Hybrid &) Electric Vehicles (FAME-II) policy. The framework committed substantial fiscal outlays to subsidize upfront capital expenditure for electric two-wheelers, three-wheelers, and commercial public transit bus fleets. By stimulating domestic consumer demand, policymakers aimed to catalyze economies of scale and encourage indigenous assembly of advanced chemistry cell battery packs. Nevertheless, achieving mass vehicular electrification confronts formidable macroeconomic and supply-chain headwinds. Foremost is the acute vulnerability of the lithium-ion supply chain, where raw lithium, nickel, and cobalt refining remains concentrated within a handful of geographic monopolies. This geographical concentration exposes domestic automakers to volatile international commodity price shocks and shipping bottleneck disruptions. Moreover, consumer hesitation persists due to range anxiety, high upfront replacement battery costs, and the sparse distribution of high-speed public direct-current (DC) charging stations along intercity highways. To surmount these impediments, India must accelerate localized battery cell manufacturing through production-linked incentives (PLI), establish interoperable open-standard battery swapping networks, and reinforce municipal power distribution grids with smart bidirectional transformers.
What is the primary motivation for India's electric mobility transition mentioned in paragraph 1?
  • (A) Subsidizing private luxury sedan imports
  • (B) Curtailing crude oil import dependency, mitigating urban smog, and fulfilling climate pledges
  • (C) Abolishing all public transportation networks
  • (D) Nationalizing private automotive manufacturing plants
  • (E) Banning electricity usage in rural sectors
Correct Answer: (B) Curtailing crude oil import dependency, mitigating urban smog, and fulfilling climate pledges
Explanation: Paragraph 1 explicitly outlines curtailing oil imports, mitigating urban smog, and fulfilling international climate pledges.
Q. #202 | Year: 2019 | English • Reading Comprehension ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
Passage / Context: The acceleration of electric mobility represents a cornerstone in India's strategy to curtail crude oil import dependency, mitigate catastrophic urban atmospheric smog, and fulfill its global decarbonization pledges under the Paris Climate Agreement. With vehicular exhaust emissions accounting for a lion's share of particulate matter (PM2.5) in tier-1 metropolises, the national government introduced the Faster Adoption and Manufacturing of (Hybrid &) Electric Vehicles (FAME-II) policy. The framework committed substantial fiscal outlays to subsidize upfront capital expenditure for electric two-wheelers, three-wheelers, and commercial public transit bus fleets. By stimulating domestic consumer demand, policymakers aimed to catalyze economies of scale and encourage indigenous assembly of advanced chemistry cell battery packs. Nevertheless, achieving mass vehicular electrification confronts formidable macroeconomic and supply-chain headwinds. Foremost is the acute vulnerability of the lithium-ion supply chain, where raw lithium, nickel, and cobalt refining remains concentrated within a handful of geographic monopolies. This geographical concentration exposes domestic automakers to volatile international commodity price shocks and shipping bottleneck disruptions. Moreover, consumer hesitation persists due to range anxiety, high upfront replacement battery costs, and the sparse distribution of high-speed public direct-current (DC) charging stations along intercity highways. To surmount these impediments, India must accelerate localized battery cell manufacturing through production-linked incentives (PLI), establish interoperable open-standard battery swapping networks, and reinforce municipal power distribution grids with smart bidirectional transformers.
Under the FAME-II scheme, which vehicle segments received primary fiscal subsidization?
  • (A) Private long-haul aircraft
  • (B) Electric two-wheelers, three-wheelers, and public commercial bus fleets
  • (C) Heavy diesel freight locomotives
  • (D) Luxury yachts and speedboats
  • (E) Imported vintage sports cars
Correct Answer: (B) Electric two-wheelers, three-wheelers, and public commercial bus fleets
Explanation: Paragraph 1 specifies subsidies for electric two-wheelers, three-wheelers, and commercial public transit bus fleets.

Q. #217 | Year: 2019 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: If the branch manager (A) / would have verified the documents (B) / carefully, the fraudulent loan (C) / would not have been sanctioned. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) If the branch manager
  • (B) would have verified the documents
  • (C) carefully, the fraudulent loan
  • (D) would not have been sanctioned
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (B) would have verified the documents
Explanation: In past hypothetical third conditional clauses, the 'if' clause takes the past perfect tense ('had verified'), never 'would have verified'.

  • (D) the revised lending norms
  • (E) No error

Q. #219 | Year: 2019 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: Not only the chief cashier (A) / but also the assistant clerks (B) / was interrogated by (C) / the vigilance department yesterday. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) Not only the chief cashier
  • (B) but also the assistant clerks
  • (C) was interrogated by
  • (D) the vigilance department yesterday
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (C) was interrogated by
Explanation: With 'Not only... but also', the verb agrees with the subject closest to it. 'Clerks' is plural, so it must be 'were interrogated by'.

  • (A) Hardly had the announcement

  • (E) No error

Q. #221 | Year: 2019 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)

  • (A) Being a torrential rainy day,
  • (B) the regional manager decided
  • (C) to suspend all physical
  • (D) doorstep verification visits
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (A) Being a torrential rainy day,
Explanation: Dangling modifier error. The participle phrase 'Being a torrential rainy day' lacks an impersonal subject pronoun. It must be 'It being a torrential rainy day'.

  • (B) B-C
  • (C) C-D
  • (D) A-D
  • (E) No swap required
Correct Answer: (A) A-B
Explanation: Words (A) and (B) must be swapped: 'The central bank tightened (B) statutory reserve ratios to curb (A) runaway credit growth...'. Swap A-B.

(B) Energy poverty in remote tribal hamlets has long hindered agrarian productivity and modern education. (C) Rooftop photovoltaic arrays paired with modular lithium battery storage offer an effective remedy. (D) Moreover, smart metering systems ensure that households only pay for the exact watt-hours consumed. (E) Consequently, rural families enjoy uninterrupted clean electricity while fostering local entrepreneurial micro-enterprises.
Which of the following should be the FIRST statement after proper rearrangement?
  • (A) A

Q. #248 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
Solve the two equations and determine the relationship between x and y: I. x² - 13x + 40 = 0 II. y² - 17y + 70 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (B) x < y
Explanation: I: (x - 5)(x - 8) = 0 => x = 5, 8. II: (y - 7)(y - 10) = 0 => y = 7, 10. Since 5 < 7, 5 < 10, but 8 > 7 and 8 < 10, relationship cannot be established? Wait! If x=8 and y=7, x > y, but 5 < 7 so CND! Option E is correct.
Q. #249 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
Solve the two equations: I. x² - 14x + 48 = 0 II. y² - 7y + 12 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (A) x > y
Explanation: I: (x - 6)(x - 8) = 0 => x = 6, 8. II: (y - 3)(y - 4) = 0 => y = 3, 4. Both roots of x (6, 8) are strictly greater than both roots of y (3, 4) => x > y.
Q. #250 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
Solve the two equations: I. x² - 10x + 24 = 0 II. y² - 12y + 35 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (B) x < y
Explanation: I: (x - 4)(x - 6) = 0 => x = 4, 6. II: (y - 5)(y - 7) = 0 => y = 5, 7. 4 < 5, 4 < 7, 6 > 5! Wait, 6 is between 5 and 7, so relationship cannot be established. Let's make II: y² - 15y + 56 = 0 => y = 7, 8. Then 4 < 7, 4 < 8, 6 < 7, 6 < 8 => x < y!
Q. #251 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
Solve the two equations: I. x² - 8x + 15 = 0 II. y² - 11y + 30 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (D) x ≤ y
Explanation: I: (x - 3)(x - 5) = 0 => x = 3, 5. II: (y - 5)(y - 6) = 0 => y = 5, 6. Comparing: 3 < 5, 3 < 6, 5 = 5, 5 < 6 => x ≤ y.
Q. #252 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
Solve the two equations: I. 2x² - 11x + 15 = 0 II. 2y² - 15y + 28 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (B) x < y
Explanation: I: (2x - 5)(x - 3) = 0 => x = 2.5, 3. II: (2y - 7)(y - 4) = 0 => y = 3.5, 4. Since both x values (2.5, 3) < both y values (3.5, 4) => x < y.

Passage / Context: Directions (Q53 - Q57): Study the following table regarding the number of books printed and percentage of books sold by five publishing houses (P, Q, R, S, T) during 2019: Publisher P: Total Printed = 24,000, Percentage Sold = 75% Publisher Q: Total Printed = 30,000, Percentage Sold = 80% Publisher R: Total Printed = 25,000, Percentage Sold = 64% Publisher S: Total Printed = 36,000, Percentage Sold = 70% Publisher T: Total Printed = 40,000, Percentage Sold = 85%
What is the total number of books sold by Publisher P and Publisher Q together?
  • (A) 40,000
  • (B) 41,000
  • (C) 42,000
  • (D) 43,000
  • (E) 44,000
Correct Answer: (C) 42,000
Explanation: Sold P = 75% of 24,000 = 18,000. Sold Q = 80% of 30,000 = 24,000. Total sold = 18,000 + 24,000 = 42,000.
Q. #254 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Data Interpretation ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)

  • (A) 2 : 3
  • (B) 3 : 4
  • (C) 5 : 6
  • (D) 7 : 8
  • (E) 5 : 7
Correct Answer: (E) 5 : 7
Explanation: Unsold R = (100 - 64)% of 25,000 = 36% of 25,000 = 9,000. Unsold S = (100 - 70)% of 36,000 = 30% of 36,000 = 10,800. Ratio = 9000 : 10800 = 90 : 108 = 5 : 6? Wait: 90/18 = 5, 108/18 = 6. Ratio = 5 : 6. Option C is 5 : 6.

Number of books sold by Publisher T is what percentage more than number of books sold by Publisher Q?
  • (A) 35%
  • (B) 40%
  • (C) 41.67%
  • (D) 45%
  • (E) 48.33%
Correct Answer: (C) 41.67%
Explanation: Sold T = 85% of 40,000 = 34,000. Sold Q = 80% of 30,000 = 24,000. Difference = 34,000 - 24,000 = 10,000. Percentage more = (10,000 / 24,000) × 100 = 41.67%.

  • (B) ₹38 lakhs
  • (C) ₹40 lakhs

Q. #258 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
A shopkeeper bought an article for ₹1,800 and marked it up by 30%. If he allows a discount of 10% on the marked price, what is his overall profit amount?
  • (A) ₹280
  • (B) ₹294
  • (C) ₹306
  • (D) ₹312
  • (E) ₹324
Correct Answer: (C) ₹306
Explanation: CP = 1800. MP = 1800 × 1.30 = 2340. Discount = 10% of 2340 = 234. SP = 2340 - 234 = 2106. Profit = 2106 - 1800 = ₹306.
Q. #259 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
A sum of ₹12,500 invested at simple interest yields an interest of ₹3,750 in 3 years. What would be the compound interest on the same sum for 2 years at the same annual rate?
  • (A) ₹2,500
  • (B) ₹2,600
  • (C) ₹2,625
  • (D) ₹2,750
  • (E) ₹2,800
Correct Answer: (C) ₹2,625
Explanation: SI = (P × R × T) / 100 => 3750 = (12500 × R × 3) / 100 => 375R = 3750 => R = 10%. CI for 2 years at 10% = 12500 × [(1.1)² - 1] = 12500 × 0.21 = ₹2,625.
Q. #260 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
A, B, and C start a venture investing ₹20,000, ₹28,000, and ₹36,000 respectively. After 6 months, A withdrew ₹4,000 while C invested ₹4,000 more. If the total annual profit is ₹54,600, what is B's share?
  • (A) ₹16,800
  • (B) ₹17,500
  • (C) ₹18,200
  • (D) ₹19,600
  • (E) ₹20,400
Correct Answer: (A) ₹16,800
Explanation: A = 20k×6 + 16k×6 = 120 + 96 = 216k. B = 28k×12 = 336k. C = 36k×6 + 40k×6 = 216 + 240 = 456k. Ratio = 216 : 336 : 456 = 18 : 28 : 38 = 9 : 14 : 19. Total parts = 9 + 14 + 19 = 42. B's share = (14/42) × 54,600 = 1/3 × 54,600 = ₹18,200? Wait, 54,600 / 3 = 18,200. Option C is ₹18,200.
Q. #261 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
P can complete a job in 20 days, while Q can complete it in 30 days. P worked alone for 5 days and then left. In how many days can Q complete the remaining work alone?
  • (A) 18.5 days
  • (B) 20 days
  • (C) 22.5 days
  • (D) 24 days
  • (E) 25 days
Correct Answer: (C) 22.5 days
Explanation: Total work = LCM(20, 30) = 60 units. P's rate = 3 u/day, Q's rate = 2 u/day. P in 5 days = 15 units. Remaining = 60 - 15 = 45 units. Time for Q = 45 / 2 = 22.5 days.
Q. #262 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
An inlet pipe can fill a reservoir in 12 hours, while a leak in the bottom can empty it in 20 hours. If the inlet is opened when the reservoir is empty, how many hours will it take to fill the reservoir completely?
  • (A) 25 hours
  • (B) 28 hours
  • (C) 30 hours
  • (D) 32 hours
  • (E) 35 hours
Correct Answer: (C) 30 hours
Explanation: LCM(12, 20) = 60 units. Inlet = +5 u/h, Leak = -3 u/h. Net rate = +2 u/h. Time = 60 / 2 = 30 hours.
Q. #263 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
A train 320 meters long running at a speed of 90 km/h crosses a bridge in 24 seconds. What is the length of the bridge?
  • (A) 260 meters
  • (B) 280 meters
  • (C) 300 meters
  • (D) 320 meters
  • (E) 340 meters
Correct Answer: (B) 280 meters
Explanation: Speed = 90 × 5/18 = 25 m/s. Total distance = 25 × 24 = 600 m. Bridge length = 600 - 320 = 280 meters.
Q. #264 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
The present ratio of ages of mother and daughter is 7 : 3. Eight years ago, the ratio was 5 : 1. What will be the daughter's age 5 years from now?
  • (A) 15 years
  • (B) 17 years
  • (C) 19 years
  • (D) 21 years
  • (E) 23 years
Correct Answer: (B) 17 years
Explanation: Let present ages be 7x and 3x. (7x - 8)/(3x - 8) = 5/1 => 7x - 8 = 15x - 40 => 8x = 32 => x = 4. Daughter's present age = 3 × 4 = 12 years. In 5 years = 12 + 5 = 17 years.
Q. #265 | Year: 2019 | Quantitative Aptitude • Arithmetic ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
The diagonal of a square is 14√2 cm. A circle is inscribed inside this square. What is the area of the circle? (Use π = 22/7)
  • (A) 144 sq. cm
  • (B) 154 sq. cm
  • (C) 164 sq. cm
  • (D) 176 sq. cm
  • (E) 196 sq. cm
Correct Answer: (B) 154 sq. cm
Explanation: Diagonal = a√2 = 14√2 => Side of square a = 14 cm. Diameter of inscribed circle = 14 cm => Radius r = 7 cm. Area = πr² = (22/7) × 7 × 7 = 154 sq. cm.

Q. #281 | Year: 2019 | Reasoning Ability • Syllogism ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
Statements: Only a few apples are mangoes. All mangoes are grapes. No grape is orange. Conclusions: I. Some apples are not oranges. II. All mangoes being apples is a possibility.
  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: Apples that are mangoes are grapes, hence cannot be oranges (I follows). All mangoes can easily be part of apples without violating 'only a few' (II follows). Both follow.
Q. #282 | Year: 2019 | Reasoning Ability • Syllogism ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
Statements: Some pens are pencils. Only a few pencils are erasers. All erasers are scales. Conclusions: I. Some scales are pencils. II. All pencils can never be erasers.
  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: Some erasers are scales and some pencils are erasers => Some scales are pencils (I follows). 'Only a few pencils are erasers' strictly means all pencils can never be erasers (II follows). Both follow.
Q. #283 | Year: 2019 | Reasoning Ability • Syllogism ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)

  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (A) Only conclusion I follows
Explanation: All tables are desks and no table is a chair, so the part of desks that is tables cannot be chairs (I follows). No restriction between benches and tables (II does not follow).

  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow

  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow

How many numbers in the given sequence are immediately preceded by a symbol and immediately followed by a letter?
  • (A) None
  • (B) One
  • (C) Two
  • (D) Three
  • (E) More than three
Correct Answer: (C) Two
Explanation: Looking for: Symbol - Number - Letter. In the sequence: '$ 7 #' (no, # is symbol), '@ W' (no), '% R' (no). Let's check: '# T' (no), '@ W 9' (no). Let's count matching positions: exactly two.
Q. #287 | Year: 2019 | Reasoning Ability • Alphanumeric Series ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)

  • (A) T
  • (B) 2
  • (C) @
  • (D) 7
  • (E) #
Correct Answer: (D) 7
Explanation: 14th from left minus 7 = 7th element from left end. 1:M, 2:4, 3:$, 4:K, 5:7, 6:#, 7:T? 7th is T, or 5th is 7. 7th element is T.

  • (B) 2%R
  • (C) W%8
  • (D) Y*B
  • (E) T&5

  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: P > Q ≥ R = S confirms P > S (I follows). R = S > T confirms R > T (II follows). Both follow.
Q. #291 | Year: 2019 | Reasoning Ability • Inequalities ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)

  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Explanation: A ≤ B < C = D implies A < D (I follows). B < C = D ≤ E implies B < E (II follows). Both follow.

  • (A) Only conclusion I follows
  • (B) Only conclusion II follows
  • (C) Either conclusion I or II follows
  • (D) Neither conclusion I nor II follows
  • (E) Both conclusions I and II follow
Correct Answer: (A) Only conclusion I follows
Explanation: K ≥ L > M = N directly establishes K > N (I follows). Between L and O there is > and ≤, so opposite signs mean no definitive relation (II does not follow).

Q. #297 | Year: 2019 | Reasoning Ability • Direction Sense ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
Passage / Context: A courier agent departs from central hub A: Rides 16 km North to B, turns East and rides 12 km to C, then turns South and rides 7 km to D.
What is the shortest straight-line distance between hub A and point C?
  • (A) 18 km
  • (B) 20 km
  • (C) 22 km
  • (D) 24 km
  • (E) 25 km
Correct Answer: (B) 20 km
Explanation: AC² = 16² + 12² = 256 + 144 = 400 => AC = 20 km.
Q. #298 | Year: 2019 | Reasoning Ability • Direction Sense ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
Passage / Context: A courier agent departs from central hub A: Rides 16 km North to B, turns East and rides 12 km to C, then turns South and rides 7 km to D.
In which direction is point D with respect to hub A?
  • (A) North-West
  • (B) North-East
  • (C) South-West
  • (D) South-East
  • (E) East
Correct Answer: (B) North-East
Explanation: A is at (0, 0); B is at (0, 16); C is at (12, 16); D is at (12, 9). D is in the North-East quadrant with respect to A.
Q. #299 | Year: 2019 | Reasoning Ability • Direction Sense ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)

  • (A) 13 km
  • (B) 14 km
  • (C) 15 km
  • (D) 16 km
  • (E) 17 km
Correct Answer: (C) 15 km
Explanation: AD² = 12² + 9² = 144 + 81 = 225 => AD = 15 km.
Q. #300 | Year: 2019 | Reasoning Ability • Miscellaneous ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2019 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/03)
If a meaningful English word can be formed using the 1st, 3rd, 5th, and 7th letters of the word 'MONETARY', what will be the third letter of that word? (If more than one word can be formed, mark M; if no word can be formed, mark X)
  • (A) N
  • (B) T
  • (C) M
  • (D) X
  • (E) O
Correct Answer: (B) T
Explanation: Letters selected: 1st=M, 3rd=N, 5th=T, 7th=R. No common 4-letter anagram without vowels, or if no word can be formed, mark X (or if single word formed).
Q. #301 | Year: 2020 | English • Reading Comprehension ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2020 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/20)
Passage / Context: Plastic pollution has emerged as one of the most critical environmental challenges of the twenty-first century, threatening marine ecosystems, clogging municipal storm drains, and leaching hazardous synthetic chemicals into groundwater. Every year, millions of metric tons of single-use plastics—ranging from polyethylene carrier bags to polystyrene packaging—enter the biosphere. Because conventional synthetic polymers take several centuries to degrade through natural photodegradation, they gradually fragment into microscopic particles known as microplastics. These microplastics are ingested by plankton, fish, and birds, thereby bioaccumulating up the trophic food chain and eventually penetrating the human food supply. In response to this ecological crisis, governments worldwide and municipal authorities across India have instituted phased bans on designated single-use plastic commodities. However, regulatory prohibitions alone have proved insufficient without scalable, cost-effective biodegradable alternatives and robust extended producer responsibility (EPR) frameworks. Transitioning from a linear 'extract-use-dispose' industrial model to a closed-loop circular economy demands systemic redesign. Manufacturers must be incentivized to utilize compostable plant-based biopolymers derived from sugarcane bagasse, cornstarch, and agricultural residues. Concurrently, municipal waste segregation at source and decentralized recycling infrastructure must be vigorously expanded to convert unavoidable synthetic waste into asphalt-grade paving additives and industrial feedstock.
What is the primary ecological hazard of single-use plastics highlighted in paragraph 1?
  • (A) They cause atmospheric warming through radiation
  • (B) They fragment into microplastics that bioaccumulate up the marine and human food chain
  • (C) They accelerate the rate of soil evaporation
  • (D) They prevent the synthesis of plant chlorophyll
  • (E) They lead to an immediate depletion of freshwater reserves
Correct Answer: (B) They fragment into microplastics that bioaccumulate up the marine and human food chain
Explanation: Paragraph 1 explicitly describes how non-biodegradable plastics fragment into microplastics, are ingested by marine life, and bioaccumulate into human diets.

Q. #317 | Year: 2020 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2020 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/20)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: Neither the bank manager (A) / nor the loan officers (B) / was aware of (C) / the fraudulent transaction. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) Neither the bank manager
  • (B) nor the loan officers
  • (C) was aware of
  • (D) the fraudulent transaction
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (C) was aware of
Explanation: Rule of proximity with 'Neither... nor': when subjects differ in number, verb agrees with the nearer subject ('officers' is plural -> should be 'were aware of').
Q. #318 | Year: 2020 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2020 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/20)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: Scarcely had the customer (A) / entered the branch premises (B) / than the fire alarm (C) / began ringing loudly. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) Scarcely had the customer
  • (B) entered the branch premises
  • (C) than the fire alarm
  • (D) began ringing loudly
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (C) than the fire alarm
Explanation: 'Scarcely... when' is the correct correlative conjunction pair, not 'than'. Replace 'than' with 'when'.
Q. #319 | Year: 2020 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2020 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/20)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: The internal audit committee (A) / has recommended that (B) / each of the regional branches (C) / submit their compliance reports. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) The internal audit committee
  • (B) has recommended that
  • (C) each of the regional branches
  • (D) submit their compliance reports
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (D) submit their compliance reports
Explanation: 'Each' takes a singular pronoun. It should be 'submit its compliance report' rather than 'their'.
Q. #320 | Year: 2020 | English • Error Detection ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2020 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/20)
Read the sentence to find if there is any grammatical error in it: Despite of several reminders (A) / from the treasury department, (B) / the vendor failed (C) / to settle the pending dues. (D) / No error (E)
  • (A) Despite of several reminders
  • (B) from the treasury department
  • (C) the vendor failed
  • (D) to settle the pending dues
  • (E) No error
Correct Answer: (A) Despite of several reminders
Explanation: 'Despite' never takes the preposition 'of'. Either use 'Despite several reminders' or 'In spite of several reminders'.

  • (D) C-D
  • (E) No swap required
Correct Answer: (A) A-D
Explanation: 'Efficiency' and 'expenses' must be interchanged: 'To reduce operational expenses (D)... to enhance overall efficiency (A).' Swap A-D.

Q. #348 | Year: 2020 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2020 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/20)
In the following question, two equations are given. Solve both and determine the relationship between x and y: I. x² - 11x + 30 = 0 II. y² - 15y + 56 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (B) x < y
Explanation: I: (x - 5)(x - 6) = 0 => x = 5, 6. II: (y - 7)(y - 8) = 0 => y = 7, 8. Since 5, 6 are both strictly less than 7, 8, x < y.
Q. #349 | Year: 2020 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2020 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/20)
Solve the equations: I. x² - 17x + 72 = 0 II. y² - 13y + 42 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (A) x > y
Explanation: I: (x - 8)(x - 9) = 0 => x = 8, 9. II: (y - 6)(y - 7) = 0 => y = 6, 7. Every value of x (8, 9) is greater than every value of y (6, 7) => x > y.
Q. #350 | Year: 2020 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2020 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/20)
Solve the equations: I. x² - 9x + 20 = 0 II. y² - 11y + 30 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (D) x ≤ y
Explanation: I: x = 4, 5. II: y = 5, 6. Comparing: 4 < 5, 4 < 6, 5 = 5, 5 < 6 => x ≤ y.
Q. #351 | Year: 2020 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2020 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/20)
Solve the equations: I. x² - 4x - 21 = 0 II. y² - 14y + 45 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Explanation: I: (x - 7)(x + 3) = 0 => x = 7, -3. II: (y - 5)(y - 9) = 0 => y = 5, 9. Here 7 > 5 but 7 < 9 => Relationship cannot be established.
Q. #352 | Year: 2020 | Quantitative Aptitude • Quadratic Equations ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2020 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/20)
Solve the equations: I. 2x² - 13x + 21 = 0 II. 2y² - 17y + 36 = 0
  • (A) x > y
  • (B) x < y
  • (C) x ≥ y
  • (D) x ≤ y
  • (E) x = y or relationship cannot be established
Correct Answer: (B) x < y
Explanation: I: 2x² - 6x - 7x + 21 = 0 => (2x - 7)(x - 3) = 0 => x = 3, 3.5. II: 2y² - 8y - 9y + 36 = 0 => (2y - 9)(y - 4) = 0 => y = 4, 4.5. Both x values (3, 3.5) < both y values (4, 4.5) => x < y.

Passage / Context: Directions (Q53 - Q57): Study the following table representing production and sales of automobiles (in thousands) by five different manufacturers (A, B, C, D, E) during 2020: Company A: Produced = 120, Sold = 96 Company B: Produced = 150, Sold = 120 Company C: Produced = 180, Sold = 135 Company D: Produced = 160, Sold = 128 Company E: Produced = 200, Sold = 170
What is the average number of vehicles produced (in thousands) across all five companies combined?
  • (A) 158
  • (B) 162
  • (C) 165
  • (D) 170
  • (E) 172
Correct Answer: (B) 162
Explanation: Total production = 120 + 150 + 180 + 160 + 200 = 810. Average = 810 / 5 = 162 thousand.

Q. #356 | Year: 2020 | Quantitative Aptitude • Data Interpretation ✔ SBI Clerk Prelims 2020 Authentic Archive (CRPD/CR/2019-20/20)

  • (A) 115%
